the A3 capability cell now names W424, the ST rows name the instrument register and its maturity reporting, and the data-migration posture is the third pass's own (the outgoing-PDC book accumulates into Bills Payable transactionally from go-live, like the BR side); ## 2026-09-16 (c) — Virtual gemba walk & virtual time-and-motion analysis (by direction: check whether the org structure and headcount is optimal): 07-methodology/virtual-gemba-walk.py shipped — a read-only analyzer that walks the 5,427-workflow corpus as a process floor and prices each chartered role's designed workload against the official TO's headcount — findings: the org's shape is corroborated by its own process measurements, with specific thin-spot and breadth tensions named for conscious review, and no evidence of structural over- or under-sizing at the org level — the tool: two modes, `walk` (per-VS touch time from step-duration midpoints, automated-step share, handoff density (adjacent-step Role (R) changes), approval-gate density, distinct executing roles, and corpus-wide role→role motion paths) and `motion` (per-role annual demand = step-duration share × events/year via a documented cadence ladder vs capacity = TO §5.3 HC / §7.2 ×200-store / §7.3 ×4-DC complements × net-hours assumptions, with utilization bands); population-pinned at the canonical 5,427/511/5,800/600 so a moved corpus invalidates the analysis loudly (exit 1); interpretation guardrails printed every run (R-cell naming is accountability, not timesheet actuals; wave-31 exercise-through-broader-titles contract; unparseable-cadence workflows excluded from annualized figures — 53% parse coverage) — the walk findings: 23,099 steps across 5,427 workflows, 74% designed-automated, ~34 min designed touch per workflow, corpus-wide approval-gate density ~85% (a deliberately control-heavy design), and the operation's motion paths concentrated exactly where the org puts its people — the store floor (Customer↔Sales Associate↔Cashier↔Store Manager↔Department Supervisor handoffs dominate; CSR↔Stock Associate the in-store services spine) — the motion findings: recurring-load demand concentrates in the field (Store Manager, Sales Associates, Stock Associates, Department Supervisors, Cashiers carry the per-store daily/weekly cadences the 5,800-person field complement exists for), the HQ picture corroborates the TO where it is deliberately thin and flags where a single pair of shoulders carries the designed load — AR Supervisor (HC 1) and AR & Credit Manager (HC 1) both read hot on the O2C chain's design-load, Treasury Analyst (3) and Pricing Analyst (4) similar — while the cold list is dominated by VP/strategic titles whose workload rides the A (approval) column and program governance rather than step-level R execution, exactly the wave-31 exercise-through-broader-titles contract — verdict recorded on the analysis record: no evidence of structural over- or under-sizing at the org level (the 6,911 complement is field-weighted as the demand is field-weighted; HQ 511 sits inside the reality-check's adopted 440–515 band with the thin spots above the candidates for any future rebalance review, not a rebalance trigger by themselves) — the tool is registered in the methodology index and the root-README tree (Checks 63/75) but deliberately NOT wired into validate-repo.sh: its utilization baselines legitimately move with every gap-fill batch, so pinning them would churn the validator without adding drift protection — read-only, exits nonzero if the canonical populations it asserts no longer hold. No corpus, generator, PA, or catalog files touched in any way. CHANGELOG entry added (exempt as frozen history). ## 2026-09-14 (q) — Role–workflow coverage matrix shipped: the role ↔ workflow mapping is now a generated, guarded artifact — 07-methodology/generate-role-coverage.py + 01-model-company/role-coverage-matrix.md (11,151 resolved actors over 5,427 workflows; deterministic --check byte-verify)

**The gap.** The role ↔ workflow mapping existed distributed in the corpus (every PA's Owner / Participants / Steps-table RACI fields, ~16,400 role mentions) and joined to the org via the TO §5.3 register's canonical-vocabulary rule — but no consolidated per-role coverage view existed. This wave ships it as a generator + generated artifact in the repo's deterministic-generator discipline.

**The generator (`07-methodology/generate-role-coverage.py`).** Parses all 569 PA files' workflow-level Owner/Participants fields and per-step Role (R) / Role (A) columns, the criticality register's confirmed Tier membership (both the `## Tier N` sections and the `### Tier N Additions` waves — the first parse drafted the additions as Tier 3 and the byte-check review caught it before ship), and the official TO (§5.3 register, §7.2 store roster, §7.3 DC roster). Resolution layers, in order: §5.3 register (incl. C-suite and role aliases — CFO/Controller/VP Merch/VP Supply Chain/DPO/S&OP Lead/LP Officer/Category Manager classes) → field rosters → IT product-model seats (the §5.3 IT row is by-reference, so the operating model's seats are mapped explicitly) → department-level actors (the department or a generic mid-management form named as performer — "Finance", "Finance Manager", "Treasury" — bucketed per §5.1 department) → system & automated actors (ERP/EBS/POS/WMS/IAP/agents) → governance bodies → generic workforce → external counterparties → an **uncharted watchlist** (10,829 low-frequency step-cell variant forms reported honestly, top-60 by coverage tabled, the tail aggregated — the promotion path into the canonical vocabulary runs through the normal role-vocabulary governance). Cells that do not resolve whole are split on commas and slashes with canonical slash-titles ("Sustainability / ESG Manager") resolving whole first. Tier mix is counted per **unique workflow** touched (owner/participant/step events are separately columned). Deterministic: no timestamps, fully sorted; `--check` byte-compares the shipped file (regenerate after any PA/role/register change).

**Data integrity asserted at every run:** all 5,427 workflows have exactly one Owner; all 5,427 resolve to exactly one confirmed Tier (1,396 / 3,296 / 758 over the 5,450-row register).

## 2026-09-14 (o) — Two-tier shape optimization: the org follows the doctrine — IT team shape conformed to the max-EBS sourcing model (OM v3.12, sourcing model v3.1, official TO v2.2, reality-check banner, methodology index) — the doctrine changed the work IT does; this wave changes the shape of the org that does it — zero net headcount (122 FTE / 17 teams; 66 domain + 56 platform/CIO unchanged; HQ 511 / total 6,911 unchanged)

**The design question.** The 2026-09-14 two-tier doctrine (*if it's in Oracle EBS we use it — otherwise we build*) re-scoped what IT works on, but the org shape still carried pre-doctrine artifacts sized for the retired three-tier posture: WLI held **two** vendor-management seats sized for the superseded best-of-breed WMS + TMS vendor estate; product/vendor management remained fragmented per team including the commodity-vendor line; and the Oracle relationship was distributed across nine product POs with no single account-team interface. This wave optimizes the shape the doctrine actually pays for.

**it-product-operating-model.md → v3.12 (the substantive moves, all mix-not-size):** (1) **WLI vendor-seat conversion** — the second vendor seat (a relic of the retired BoB WMS/TMS estate) converts to a **fifth functional analyst** carrying the in-suite Oracle WMS/MSCA + Shipping/OTE configuration surface (§9.1: WLI Functional Analysts 4 → 5, Platform PM 2 → 1; team total 10, domain subtotal 66 untouched; the sizing anchor 66 + 56 = 122 and the reconciliation 171 + 17 = 188 / 4,935 + 492 = 5,427 stand); (2) **retitling and re-charter** — the §9.1 'Vendor PM' column and the §5.4 Product/Vendor Manager role become **Platform Product Manager**: one seat per configure-and-integrate team, with WLI's seat chartered as the **chain-wide Oracle Relationship & In-Suite Currency Manager** (one account-team interface, one RUP/CPU calendar, ADOP rehearsal discipline, in-suite currency KPI owner) while module-level release/roadmap intake stays with every PO, and SSP's/CCP's seats product-managing the already-built in-house platforms (POS estate; ecommerce/loyalty/gift-card) and liaising their in-house builds (store workforce; dispatch); the §5.1 configure-and-integrate blockquote re-issued to match; (3) **commodity-vendor commercial consolidation** — commercial ownership of the vendor-commodity line (LLM APIs, managed SOC, outsourced L1, hardware/licenses) consolidates in the **CIO Office vendor-portfolio analyst** (§5.3), one register and one negotiating posture, with owning teams keeping operational roadmap-intake/release-intake/SLA-escalation ownership (§8.2 cadence bullet re-worded to match); (4) WLI's §8.3 KPI row gains the in-suite currency KPI (Oracle WMS/MSCA + Shipping/OTE current within one RUP). Sizing §9.1's note documents the conversion; §9.2's +35 history left standing as the v2.0 record.

**capability-sourcing-and-engineering-model.md → v3.1:** §5's configure-and-integrate archetype row names the single Platform Product Manager seat (WLI doubling as the Oracle Relationship & In-Suite Currency Manager); §8's vendor-commodities bullet re-scoped to the operational/commercial split (operational ownership in the owning team, commercial ownership — contracts, price-escalation caps, renewal/exit clauses, tier-1 TPRM interface — with the CIO Office vendor-portfolio analyst). No gate, Register, §12 program, or sizing changes. Companion pins re-pointed to OM v3.12 (header, §13 row, footer newest-clause chain).

**optimal-table-of-organization.md → v2.2 (the official TO of record speaks two-tier):** the banner's target-state basis, the §1 two-state table row, the design-stance band note, the §5.1 IT driver text, the §5.1 band note and §11's Phase-3/guardrail wording now describe the two-tier capability-sourcing model (the 2026-09-03 'hybrid' naming retained only where it records dated band-extension history); §5.2's IT paragraph gains the doctrine's shape note (Platform PM per integrate team, commodity-vendor commercial consolidation, the WLI conversion). Every figure unchanged — HQ 511 / total 6,911, IT 122 / 17 teams, the §5.3 register's 188 rows / 187 distinct role titles and all to_register_hits/to_phase_hits invariants.

**Companions.** headcount-reality-check.md STATUS banner gains the 2026-09-14 update clause with re-pointed live pins (TO v2.2, OM v3.12); 07-methodology/README.md index OM row re-pinned (v3.11 → v3.12); OM footer Downstream pins re-pointed (TO v2.2, sourcing model v3.1).

**Verification.** No PA/workflow/catalog files touched — generators unaffected by definition (re-run byte-identical, exit 0); validate-repo.sh green 0 errors / 0 warnings across all 77 checks post-edit. Canonical totals unchanged: 188 VS / 569 PA / 5,427 WF / 5,450 register rows (T1 1,396 / T2 3,296 / T3 758) / 808 CTL / 728 Req / 6,762 HC current — HQ 511 / 6,911 target, IT 122.

## 2026-09-14 (b) — Two-tier sourcing doctrine enacted for the EBS realization: *if it's in Oracle EBS we use it, otherwise we build* — fit-gap register re-dispositioned 76 → 79 rows, POS/ecommerce/loyalty recorded as already-built platforms (integration, not sourcing), payroll re-scoped to an in-house build (Oracle Payroll not adopted), BoB WMS/TMS/WFM/FSM rows superseded with the resolution record + sourcing-register amendment schedule (fit-gap §4)

**The decision.** Per direction received 2026-09-14, the EBS realization's sourcing posture collapses to two tiers: **(1) if the capability is in Oracle EBS, we use it — standard, configured; (2) otherwise, we build it in-house.** The best-of-breed buy tier is eliminated for capability products (vendor LLM APIs remain commodity procurement under tier-1 TPRM, not capability sourcing). Two specific calls accompany the doctrine: **the POS is already built** — "we already built the POS so it's a matter of integrating it" — and **payroll is built**: an in-house Payroll PH product replaces the Oracle-Payroll-plus-localization plan, with Core HR (PER) and the GL staying in EBS and the build posting period costing journals via the GL interface (EBS remains the ledger of record).

**Register re-disposition (fit-gap-analysis.md v1.1, 76 → 79 rows).** New counts pinned at **54 standard (31 FIT-STD + 23 FIT-CFG, 68.4%) / 2 PER / 2 EXT / 1 LOC / 5 INT / 15 BUILD — of which 5 are already-built platforms (D6 POS, D7 ecommerce, D8 gift cards, D9 loyalty, D11 marketplace) — 0 EDGE (class retired), 0 OPEN (both resolved)**. Moves: C6 warehouse execution EDGE → FIT-STD (**Oracle WMS/MSCA adopted in-suite** — the register's own "ERP vendor ships native WMS at parity" trigger answered by adoption, not assessment); new C14 transport/tender/freight FIT-STD (Shipping Execution + OTE in-suite; BoB TMS superseded); C5 forecasting/S&OP OPEN → FIT-CFG (**Oracle ASCP + Demantra** adopted — the EBS-family planning stack; license cost is FinOps, not sourcing); B10 OTM resolved in-suite; D6–D9/D11 EDGE → BUILD*(existing)*; new D13 installation-dispatch BUILD (BoB FSM row flips to build; remit — extend TPS vs new squad — at SIB/OM); F4 planogram/space BUILD (masters W314 stay EBS-governed); E-group rebuilt E1–E8: E1 Core HR FIT-STD (stays in EBS), E2 in-suite, E3 store workforce scheduling/time-capture BUILD (BoB WFM row flips), E4 time-attendance import INT, E5 in-house payroll engine BUILD (SSS/PhilHealth/Pag-IBIG, PD 851, BIR withholding — built, not localized on Oracle Payroll), E6 statutory outputs/agency files BUILD, E7 BIR compensation outputs (2316/1601-C/1604-C/alphalist) BUILD, E8 payroll costing → GL posting INT; G4 re-scoped to the **EBS-held BIR indirect-tax pack** (2307 AP-side, VAT datasets, CAS inventory list W478) — the single remaining LOC row; §6 rewritten as the statutory split (EBS-held vs payroll-built, nothing duplicated); §7 KPI gains the absolute "best-of-breed capability products: 0" gate.

**Resolution record + amendment schedule (fit-gap §4).** Eight decisions recorded (WMS adoption; ASCP/Demantra adoption; OTM resolution; TPRM unchanged; ATS/LMS unchanged; POS already-built; payroll build; workforce/dispatch new-build scope) — each inheriting the sourcing gate's §3.3 control-mapping duty through its re-dispositioned register row. The Capability Sourcing Register (§4) amendment is recorded in this entry and in fit-gap §4 as the authoritative interim decision; **the sourcing-model document rewrite (§2 tier table, §4 rows, §5 archetype wording, §8 scope) is explicitly scheduled as the next consistency wave**, so no guarded surface is silently contradicted in the interim.

**Cascade re-points (02-oracle-ebs only + navigation surfaces).** Folder README v1.1 (decision table: in-suite WMS/OTE rows, already-built platforms row, payroll-build row, foundation-models-noted-as-commodity row; principles ladder; W4 wave re-scoped "Core HR + payroll-build integration"; relationships bullet superseding the register's Buy rows with the rewrite scheduled). ebs-platform-architecture v1.1 (WMS/MSCA row adopted; OTE row in-suite; HRMS row re-scoped with **Oracle Payroll marked not-adopted**; not-adopted row extended; in-house-products roster added; POS/WMS "edges" wording trued to platforms/in-suite). module-coverage-map v1.1 (POS/ecommerce/loyalty rows re-pointed to already-built; WMS row in-suite; HR & Payroll row rebuilt; planning row Oracle VCP; family table Sell & Serve/Move & Store/People & Culture re-worded; non-adoption boundary re-worded; edge stragglers swept). integrations v1.1 (integrated-systems wording; **payroll posting row added** to the pattern register — Payroll PH → `GL_INTERFACE` via IAP with per-period journal tie-out; government-channel adapters fed by EBS and the payroll build). data-migration v1.1 (row 11: payroll YTD loads into the build, EBS receives postings not balances; W4 sequence updated). customization-governance v1.1 (ladder and CDR alternative set re-worded with EDGE retired; de-customization triggers re-pointed to the doctrine's re-evaluation discipline). Root README: Purpose carries the two-tier doctrine; tree fit-gap row re-pointed to the 79-row counts. CHANGELOG (this entry) records the interim supersession. No PA/workflow/catalog files touched — generators' outputs unchanged by definition (bpmn 569 files / 5,450 processes / 23,016 tasks / 28,466 flows / 5,450 diagrams; dmn 40 files / 79 decisions / 339 rules / 197 deferred); canonical totals unchanged 188 VS / 569 PA / 5,427 WF / 5,450 register rows (T1 1,396 / T2 3,296 / T3 758) / 808 CTL / 728 Req / 6,762 HC current — HQ 511 / 6,911 target.

**What was added and why.** The model company's planning corpus (188 VS / 569 PA / 5,427 WF / 808 CTL / 728 Req) was written platform-agnostic; the root-README structure reserved the platform-specific slots ("future: 02-06/"). SSS/PhilHealth/Pag-IBIG/PD 851, agency files, BIR compensation and indirect-tax packs), the 7 EDGE and 4 BUILD rows are all pre-decided by the sourcing register; plus 5 SIB open decisions — Oracle WMS/MSCA satisfying the register's own WMS parity trigger, ASCP/Demantra vs the in-house forecast pipeline (the single OPEN row), OTM for vendor rebates, TPRM tooling depth, ATS/LMS depth — each through the W5515 gate with its §3.3 appendices; R1–R32 orientation view; standard-first KPIs), `customization-governance.md` (CEMLI operationalized with the M-class absolutely prohibited, the extension budget — 10 EXT / 150 PER caps with retire-to-add rule, the 8-condition admissibility test, the CDR workflow on ARB/SIB bodies, technical + ADOP-safe coding standards, the CEMLI register with quarterly database-diff audit, de-customization triggers incl. the RUP-ships-it rule, KPIs), `integrations.md` (the EBS-native mechanism set — open interface tables, public APIs, ISG REST/SOAP, WF_EVENT business events, XML Gateway, BI Publisher extracts — behind the IAP-only path; a per-flow pattern register mirroring every row of the canonical integration matrix with its reconciliation control named; government-channel-adapter rule keeping statutory filings human-confirmed per the sourcing model's §12.1 agentic hard boundary; latency/batch discipline; the five-class error taxonomy; the new-interface gate), and `data-migration.md` (a 21-row object → EBS load-path register — interfaces and public APIs only, never direct writes — sequenced across the six waves with six validation gates tied to W73/W385, the historical-data strategy, and the per-wave cutover sequence).

**Doctrinal reconciliation (no sourcing-register changes).** The Capability Sourcing Register (§4) remains the single record of sourcing decisions; the EBS blueprint adds no new buys and no new builds. The "unified cloud ERP (core)" row is realized by product as EBS 12.2 (hosted private deployment — EBS is not SaaS; RA 10173 data residency fully in BuildRight control); the POS core-tier capability is executed by the POS edge under EBS item/price/tax masters because the suite ships no POS — capability scope unchanged, product realization documented; Oracle WMS/MSCA's existence *satisfies* the register's WMS re-evaluation trigger, so the parity assessment is scheduled at the SIB rather than assumed — the BoB default stands until re-affirmed.

**Cascade re-points.** Root README: Purpose paragraph re-pointed ("a unified cloud ERP core" → the EBS selection with the fit-to-standard doctrine), Out-of-Scope first bullet reworded (comparative evaluation stays out of scope; the selection is recorded, not re-argued), folder-structure tree gains the 02-oracle-ebs block (7 rows), Document-Relationships diagram's "(future: 02-06/)" cell re-pointed to `02-oracle-ebs/`. ## 2026-09-10 — Post-catalog workflow-level gap fill (batch 24): W5574 Concessionaire Connectivity Request, Approval & Independent-Circuit Governance (VS-07.1) — the owned exception path when a concessionaire wants its own internet connection; canonical totals 5,426 → 5,427 workflows / 5,449 → 5,450 register rows

**What was added and why.** A concessionaire operating-model review (the batch-3 concession-catalog lineage) found the connectivity-exception surface unowned: W177 bundles internet into the monthly 'Rent + Utilities + Commission' concessionaire invoice as a BuildRight-provided utility and W111 benchmarks internet as a store utility, W366 manages the store link with its segmented Wi-Fi, and W5520/W5418/W5421 guard the IPAM, segmentation and device onboarding boundaries — but no workflow owned the case where a concessionaire wants its own internet connection ('concessionaire internet'/'independent circuit' appear in zero PA files as owned content). **W5574 Concessionaire Connectivity Request, Approval & Independent-Circuit Governance** (PA-07.1, extending the W177 concession lineage alongside W5505–W5507) owns the full path: the default-posture rule (connectivity is a BuildRight-provided utility; an independent circuit is an exception, never a store-level decision), the segmented Wi-Fi alternative on BuildRight's own link (W366 step 4) evaluated and billed as a utility first, the eligibility gate (documented regulatory, banking or capacity need only — never convenience or cost), the technical isolation envelope (separate conduit/rise, no physical or logical crossing into any BuildRight network per W5418, DHCP confined to the concessionaire's own equipment per W5520, no BuildRight credentials), the W47 facilities works approval with landlord consent (VS-42/VS-97 master-lease discipline) and LGU permits for exterior terminations, the W117 connectivity addendum (incident demarcation keeping the unmanaged circuit off the W48 helpdesk, liability/insurance per VS-147, and the commission-integrity clause keeping every concession sale on the BuildRight POS throughput SKU so the W177 calculation never rides on connectivity choice), the W581/W71-escorted installation with IT Security isolation verification and W1409 change control for any network-side accommodation, annual re-attestation riding the W5505 catalog reconciliation, and the W62-gated exit walk-through releasing the restoration holdback only after circuit cancellation and cabling removal. Ships **directly confirmed Tier 2** (the concession-operations governance class of the W5505–W5507 siblings; the network-boundary machinery it invokes is already in place, so nothing is go-live blocking); absorbed within sized teams (OM stays 122 FTE / 17 teams; TO stays HQ 511 / 6,911 — Time-Estimate math: ~2.5 hrs/request × ~100–300 requests/yr + ~4 hrs/approval × ~10–60 approvals/yr + 15 min re-attestation per flagged agreement).

**Defect found and repaired (a surface class no prior wave had read).** A repo-wide monotonicity sweep of every `*Document Version:` footer — live version vs its `Prior vN.M` chain — found exactly one inversion: the AI-first operating guide's header had stayed at **'1.0 | 2026-09-04 | Initial issue'** while all eight batch-16–23 re-point cascades (v1.1–v1.8, each dated 2026-09-05) appended clauses labeled **'Prior'** with *higher* version numbers and *later* dates below it — the only inverted chain in the corpus (every other maintained doc keeps its newest clause leading under the live version with a strictly decreasing Prior chain). The drift was invisible to `methodology_index_hits` because the index row's own '(v1.0, 2026-09-04)' pin satisfied itself off the same stale footer — the wave-6 'anchor satisfying itself off history' failure mode, in its version-pin form — and the commit messages had called the changes 'guide v1.N' all along, so the chain clauses carried true content while the header froze. Repair: the footer re-based newest-first to **live v1.9** with its own eleventh-wave clause (the initial issue retained verbatim, demoted to the chain's end as 'Prior v1.0 (2026-09-04)'; the eight prior clauses standing as written, now in genuinely descending order); no body-content changes — the catalog triple, Tier ladder, canon tables and §KB counts were already at batch-23 state. Companion pins re-pointed: the methodology-index row to '(v1.9, 2026-09-07)' and the root-README tree row to '(v1.9)'.

**Guard extension (the gap that let the inversion live through eight batches).** validate-repo.sh **Check 72** added — document version-chain monotonicity, repo-wide: for every .md carrying a `*Document Version:` footer, every `Prior vN.M` on the footer line must be strictly below the live version (componentwise) and the chain strictly decreasing in document order, so the class is caught at the first inverted clause rather than eight batches later; CHANGELOG and the generated bpmn//dmn trees exempt, and only the footer line is scanned so body-narrative 'Prior' mentions can never false-trigger the order rule (the rule's first draft caught its own author — the eleventh-wave clause originally carried literal 'Prior v1.8' / 'Prior v1.0' mentions inside its narrative, which would have broken the decreasing-order scan on a correct footer — reworded to 'the eight prior clauses (v1.8 down to v1.1)' per the wave-9 precedent). Teeth verified by two synthetic injections: (1) the guide's live header reverted to '1.0 | 2026-09-04 | Initial issue' → caught twice (the priors-newer-than-live inversion flagged at the footer line, and `methodology_index_hits` flagging the index row's v1.9 pin against the reverted footer — the same two-face failure the original drift hid behind, now closing from both sides); (2) a synthetic 'Prior 9.9' clause injected into data-volumes-and-integrations.md's footer → caught at the exact line as at/above live. Fixtures restored byte-identical (sha256-verified). Check count **71 → 72** with the five quoted self-descriptions re-pointed (guide §8.5 canon row and both §8.5 body mentions, root-README tree row, methodology-index validate-repo row guard-listing extended with the Check 72 description); the audit-model-docs module history gains the eleventh-wave paragraph (recording why the footer-chain invariant deliberately lives in the validator, not in this module: it is a cross-document invariant, not a per-doc one).

**Defects found and repaired.** The review took up the sixth wave's one flagged-for-adjudication residual: §4.9's SSP row enumerates `+1 (VS-151)` while its workflow figure (882) only reconciles *excluding* VS-151, and DP's 213 only *including* it (DP's own seven listed VSs hold 189 on disk) — a mismatch now traced to the document's issuance itself (the v1.0 §3.2 carried SSP 873 / DP 210 with the same asymmetry: twenty reconciliation passes each trued the deltas correctly without ever re-deriving the base). **Adjudication: the mapping side is unanimous** — §4.3 lists VS-151 (Auto-ID, Barcode, RFID, Price-Tag Labeling & EAS Operations) under SSP with business owner 'Store Operations with Loss Prevention', §4.9's SSP row names VS-151 explicitly, and VS-151 sits in the Sell & Serve family while DP's seven VSs are all drawn from the Technology & Data / Governance families, so VS-151 cannot belong to DP without breaking the family partition — therefore the *workflow figures* are the wrong side. Repairs: **SSP 882 → 906** (VS-151's 24 workflows return to the team its mapping assigns) and **DP 213 → 189** in both §3.2 and §4.9. The same re-derivation exposed a **second, independent latent drift no prior wave had noticed**: the hand-maintained subtotal split sat ±3 off its own member rows (4,913 + 513 declared against member sums 4,910 + 516 pre-repair) — the split series had been carried by deltas since before v3.8 and never cross-footed to the rows it summarizes — so the subtotals are re-based to the member sums **4,913 + 513 → 4,934 + 492** (total unchanged 5,426), and the §4.9 Total row and live footer clause re-pointed to match, making the reconciliation read **171 + 17 = 188 / 4,934 + 492 = 5,426**. No team, sizing, phase, or VS-assignment changes — VS-151 was always mapped to SSP; only its workload count followed. Cascade: the OM bumped to **v3.9** with its own tenth-wave footer clause (the v3.8 clause retained as written below it); the sourcing model bumped to **v2.9** (header companion pin, §13 Related-Documents OM row and footer newest clause re-pointed to v3.9; no sourcing-rule, §12.1 ladder, §12.2 intake-figure, or program changes); the OM §13 self-pin trued to 'this v3.9 model'; the OM footer Downstream pointer re-pointed to sourcing v2.9; the reality-check STATUS banner OM pin re-pointed to v3.9; the methodology-index OM row pin re-pointed to v3.9.

The rule now also derives team → VS from the §4.1–4.8 mapping tables (188 rows parsed; CIO Office normalized), counts each VS's `## W` headers on disk, and asserts **every §3.2 per-team cell (VS and workflow), both subtotal cells against their member-row sums, and every §4.9 per-team workflow cell** against that derivation each run; the mapping-table sanity check (188 rows) guards against structure drift. Teeth verified by four synthetic injections, each caught at the exact line with the correct diagnosis: (1) the §3.2 SSP workload cell reverted to 882 → caught by the per-team derivation ('declares 882 … but the §4 mapping + disk hold 906'); (2) the Domain subtotal re-corrupted to the old 4,913 → caught twice (anchor regression on the re-pinned '**171** | **4,934**' presence pin + the subtotal-vs-total cross-foot); (3) the §4.9 DP cell reverted to 213 → caught by the per-team derivation at its line; (4) a §4 mapping row moved (VS-99 from FS to DP) → caught by three rules simultaneously (both §3.2 rows and the §4.9 DP row, each against the shifted disk derivation). Independent deep validation beyond the generators' self-checks: every process exactly one startEvent + one endEvent; every flow node covered by ≥ 1 lane with lane refs inside their own process; exactly one BPMNDiagram/BPMNPlane per process with plane ref = the process id; DI shape for every node/lane/annotation, edge for every flow/association, positive bounds, ≥ 1 waypoint per edge; DMN U-table disjointness independently re-proven across all 68 U tables (45 numeric-band tables under FEEL interval semantics with open/closed boundaries, the rest by full input-tuple uniqueness); every decision carries provenance `<description>` and a DMNShape. ## 2026-09-05 — DMN 1.3 decision layer: add generate-dmn.py and the generated dmn/ tree (40 files, 79 decisions, 339 rules) — companion to generate-bpmn.py, extracting from the workflow markdown the content that already exists as explicit rules

**What shipped.** `07-methodology/generate-dmn.py` extracts the two rule kinds the corpus already carries: (1) **rule-shaped markdown tables** (header with a criteria-like column — severity/tier/amount/scenario/category/priority — plus outcome column(s) — action/approval/SLA/escalation/authority; 14 table shapes corpus-wide): every criteria column becomes a DMN input, every other column an output (no column dropped), every row a rule; (2) **tiered money-threshold authorization rules in step prose** — the "(a) ≤ PHP X: Role A; (b) PHP X–Y: Role B; (c) > PHP Y: Role C" idiom, including the "> PHP 50K → Category Manager; > PHP 500K → VP Merchandising; > PHP 2M → CFO" escalating form, compound bounds ("> PHP 50,000 and ≤ PHP 200,000"), and ranges with unit inheritance ("PHP 10–50M" → [10000000..50000000]) — one decision table over Amount (FEEL number) with Approver output, tiers as rules. Integrity mechanics: hit policy U published only after mechanical pairwise-disjointness verification (string tables require full input-tuple uniqueness; failing sets demoted to C or deferred); documented ladder normalizations ((≤ X) followed by (PHP X–Y) assigns the shared boundary to the lower tier; escalating open lower bounds rewritten to disjoint (A..B] bands with a per-decision note); non-monetary tiers in marker lists excluded from the amount table and flagged; no partial extraction — every money-bearing segment must resolve to band+approver or the set is refused (caught in testing: an early draft emitted a wrong 2-tier slice of a 5-tier matrix); 197 ambiguous rule sets (single-threshold without a complementary tier, multi-criteria matrices, percent-only tiers) deferred rather than faked — the markdown remains source of truth. Output: one DMN 1.3 definitions per PA with full DMNDI shapes (dmn-js/Camunda-compatible); decisions carry provenance descriptions quoting the verbatim source text.

**Validation.** Generator self-checks (well-formed XML, unique ids, decisionTable structure, entry-count match, non-empty entries, DMNShape ref resolution, U-policy disjointness re-verification) exit 0; independent deep validator clean across all 40 files including an independent FEEL band re-parse re-proving disjointness for every U table; regeneration byte-identical (idempotent); validate-repo.sh green 0 errors / 0 warnings across all 70 checks with the new tree. Coverage: 79 decisions across 25 of 188 value streams (40 of 569 process areas); root README tree gains `dmn/` and `generate-dmn.py` rows; `dmn/README.md` documents conventions, coverage and the deferral policy.

## 2026-09-05 — BPMN wiring fix: correct the systematic incoming/outgoing off-by-one, eliminate prefix-clobbered dangling refs, add last-task outgoing, and extend generator validation to mirror-check wiring

**Defect family found.** Review of the shipped `bpmn/` tree found every task's `<bpmn:outgoing>` pointing at its own incoming flow (`build_process` resolved `PLACEHOLDER_OUT_{i+1}` to `flow_ids[i]`, the flow entering the task, instead of `flow_ids[i+1]`): 17,258 wrong-target outgoing refs + 5,449 processes whose last task shipped no outgoing despite the task→End flow + 1,042 dangling refs (301 outgoing / 741 incoming) in the 266 processes with ≥9 tasks across 71 files, where ascending `str.replace` let `PLACEHOLDER_OUT_1/IN_1` clobber the prefixes of `OUT_10-18/IN_10-31` leaving digit residue (e.g. W64_End17 incoming W64_Flow200 = 'Flow20'+'0'; worst case PA-17.2 W14 at 31 flows corrupts the IN_30/31 zone too). The generator's own validator stayed green because it never checked the incoming/outgoing child elements.

**Fix.** The placeholder map now assigns flow k to the outgoing ref of chain[k] and the incoming ref of chain[k+1]; replacement runs longest-placeholder-first (sorted by length descending) so prefixes can't be clobbered; every task emits an outgoing placeholder (the last-task task→End wiring is no longer omitted); an unresolved-placeholder RuntimeError guards against silent residue; and `validate_file` re-derives expected wiring from the sequenceFlows and fails on any node whose declared incoming/outgoing doesn't resolve to and exactly mirror them.

**Regeneration & re-validation.** `bpmn/` regenerated exit 0; canonical counts unchanged 569 files / 5,449 processes / 23,008 tasks / 28,457 flows / 5,449 diagrams; change surface surgical — 17,559 outgoing rewritten + 741 incoming rewritten + 5,449 last-task outgoing added, start-event outgoing and all non-wiring content byte-identical. Independent re-validation of all 569 files clean across every category (wiring resolve+mirror, lane refs/coverage/uniqueness, start/end cardinality, annotation+association, DI shapes/edges/bounds/waypoints, no duplicate ids, no placeholder residue), with the previously broken patterns spot-verified (W1_Task7 out=Flow19, W64_End17 in=Flow29, W564 15-flow and W14 31-flow chains fully wired). `bpmn/README` validation section documents the new wiring check.

*backfilled by the 2026-09-07 full-history review — commit 395538c shipped without its CHANGELOG record.*

## 2026-09-05 — BPMN 2.0 generation: add generate-bpmn.py converter and the generated bpmn/ tree (569 files, 5,449 processes)

**What shipped.** `07-methodology/generate-bpmn.py` (511 lines) parses every `PA-*.md` process-area file under `01-model-company/workflows/VS-*/`, converts each workflow block (`## W<id>. <Name>`, including `###`/`####` sub-workflows such as W5A) into a BPMN 2.0 `<process>`, and emits `bpmn/VS-<slug>/PA-<slug>.bpmn` with full `BPMNDiagram`/`BPMNPlane` DI so files open directly in any BPMN 2.0 modeler. Mapping: Trigger → labeled start event; steps-table rows → sequential tasks (`serviceTask` when Role R contains System or the Duration is Automated, else `userTask`); distinct responsible roles → lanes in first-seen order; activity + R/A/Duration → task documentation; field tables → process documentation; Controls → text annotation on the start event. The built-in validator re-parses every output and checks sequenceFlow source/target, lane flowNodeRef and DI bpmnElement resolution; exit 1 on any failure. The tree: `bpmn/` (571 files — 569 `.bpmn` mirroring the workflows tree + README.md documenting the coverage — 5,449 processes, 23,008 tasks, 28,457 sequence flows, 5,449 DI diagrams — and the conversion mapping; generated artifacts, do not hand-edit, regenerate via the script); root README tree gains `bpmn/` and `generate-bpmn.py` rows. (The wiring this commit shipped carried a systematic off-by-one — corrected by the wiring-fix entry above; tree counts and all non-wiring content are unchanged by that fix.)

(2) **the procurement-impersonation / fake-PO goods-diversion event** — 'fake PO'/'fraudulent purchase order'/'supplier impersonation' in zero corpus hits; the buyer-side mirror of W5559 (diverted payments on genuine obligations) and W5563 (fake sellers defrauding consumers): fraudsters posing as BuildRight place orders with our suppliers and collect via a carrier BuildRight never booked — no payment ever moves, so no bank recall exists and the corpus had no play for the event: the void-order posture (fraudulent orders are void ab initio with same-day written notice on authenticated channels), the supplier-notification wave with portal-only verification, the receiving hold flags on diverted PO numbers, the W839 insider check where PO formats were too precisely mimicked, or the self-verifiable order-code retrofit — **W5567** (PA-03.2, Procurement Specialist / VP Legal & Compliance); (3) **the official-channel account-takeover event** — 'account hijack'/'brand account' in zero corpus hits; W5563 owns the fake pages that only pretend to be BuildRight, W3871 customer-account ATO, and W5550 platform-side enforcement — nobody owned the takeover of BuildRight's own verified accounts, where a scam post inherits the platform's verification badge and defeats follower self-verification: the first-hour credential kill-chain (password/session/token revocation across the estate before any recovery appeal), the platform trust-&-safety recovery with corporate proof (never paid "recovery services"), the same-day follower advisory from unaffected verified channels, the victim guidance riding the W5563-class machinery, or the W53 privacy clock where the hijacker could read follower messages — **W5568** (PA-14.2, Social Media Manager / VP Legal & Compliance); (4) **the mass-commute disruption / transport-strike event** — 'transport strike'/'commute disruption' in zero corpus hits; W4255 responds to a single shuttle incident, W850/W2510 decide whether a site opens, and the W576/W848/W1387 site-protection canons target buildings — nobody owned the event where every site is undamaged yet unstaffed because the crews cannot get in: the attendance-forecast call from commute-mode profiles, the critical-role matrix (keyholders, cashiers, receiving) with no-penalty absence coding, the W4253-gated shuttle-surge and ride-share activation, the explicit service-level matrix for skeleton-crew trading, or the customer-impact decision riding the E-02 closure custody — **W5569** (PA-141.2, Transport Lead / Site Managers). All four ship with all 9 required fields + Automation + Controls (CTL-264 PA-08.2 anchor, CTL-247 PA-03.2 anchor, CTL-282 PA-14.2 anchor, CTL-654 PA-141.2 anchor) + Time Estimates, absorbed within sized teams (OM stays 122 FTE / 17 teams; TO stays HQ 511 / 6,911).

**Tier confirmations.** All four ship **directly confirmed Tier 2**: W5566 (the payment-device security class of the W1205/W5547 precedent — the PCI program and tender-outage canon own the estate and the rail, not the device event), W5567 (the procurement-fraud contingency class of W5559/W5563), W5568 (the brand-integrity channel-contingency class of W5563/W5550), and W5569 (the workforce-continuity class of W5561/W4255).

## 2026-09-05 — Batch 21: storefront-crash, brand-impersonation-scam, wallet-outage & adjacent-works workflow gap fill — W5562–W5565 added across four PA files after re-running the batch-16/17/18/19/20 edge-case sweeps across scenario families not yet probed (vehicle impact into an operating store, brand-impersonation commerce scams whose victims arrive at stores, mobile-wallet provider outages, adjacent construction damaging occupied property); the same analysis produced the custody register's eighth wave (events E-38–E-41); canonical totals now 188 VS / 569 PA / 5,418 workflows / 5,441 register rows / 808 controls

**Edge-case gaps found and filled.** Four unowned scenarios: (1) **the vehicle-impact / storefront-crash event** — 'vehicle into building'/'storefront crash' in zero corpus hits; W330's in-store emergency trigger names structural failure generically but defers event-specific execution (the E-19 fire precedent), W5555 owns the fatality scene protocol, W1450's structural assessment is quake-specific, and W653 owns our fleet crashing elsewhere — nobody owned the survivable-crash class: the crash-scene safety law (an unstable vehicle, a fuel leak and compromised glazing are hazards before they are evidence), the liability ordering (the driver's compulsory third-party liability and own-damage cover is the primary claim — BuildRight's property loss runs as subrogation, not the other way round), the red-tag structural discipline before any zone reopens (the W5537 BFP-clearance analog for impact damage), the PNP traffic-investigation ownership with a no-fault-statements rule, or the bollard/barrier retrofit feedback into the site-standards register — **W5562** (PA-147.2, Store Manager / Head of Customer Safety); (2) **the brand-impersonation commerce-scam event** — 'fake seller'/'scam listing' in zero corpus hits; W3271/W3272 own the monitoring and takedown machinery, W3268 the infringement action against identifiable abusers, W5557 the recruitment-fraud variant whose victims also arrive at BuildRight's door, and VS-125 watches customer-side payment fraud — nobody owned the commerce-scam event itself: the victims-arrive-at-our-stores protocol (people who paid a fake seller presenting at a service desk expecting goods — never blamed, always routed to recall/report channels), the same-day public advisory with a single verification source of truth, the one-simultaneous-wave takedown discipline, the e-wallet recall guidance and PNP-ACG referral, or the data-harvesting variant that triggers the W53 breach-assessment clock — **W5563** (PA-100.2, IP Counsel); (3) **the mobile-wallet platform-outage event** — one tender-outage canon per rail: W5547 owns the card-scheme/acquirer rail (E-24) and W535 the store-connectivity layer, but the wallet-provider rail (GCash/Maya-class QR/NFC authorization) had no owner — the counterparty, authorization rail, fallback mechanics (a clean tender-type suspension while cards and cash keep working), settlement catch-up (the provider's T+1 payout statement, not acquirer batches), and fraud window (phished-OTP social engineering and double-scan attempts rather than offline-auth abuse) are all distinct: nobody owned the rail-diagnosis protocol, the tender-type directive flag, the no-manual-receipt double-charge rule, or the stranded-authorization sweep against the provider statement — **W5564** (PA-08.1, Head of Customer Service); ## 2026-09-04 — AI-First Operating Guide added: the complete ERP-agnostic, sourcing-neutral operating playbook synthesized from the corpus — ten operating laws, six-layer architecture on the knowledge plane, capability-contract doctrine, the agent factory, the Enterprise Knowledge Base as persistent memory, the no-confusion decision system, and the seven-phase adoption path; canonical totals unchanged (188 VS / 569 PA / 5,388 workflows / 5,411 register rows / 808 controls)

**Guide shipped.** [`07-methodology/ai-first-operating-guide.md`](07-methodology/ai-first-operating-guide.md) (v1.0) synthesizes the operating doctrine an adopting company of similar industry runs, with the BuildRight corpus as the reference instantiation of every construct: the **ten operating laws** (each with its enforcement mechanism — one truth, one owner, contracts-not-products, configure/buy/build, agents-as-workers, earned autonomy, human-owned judgment/law/money, evidence, KB-or-it-doesn't-exist, no-one-ever-stuck); the **six-layer reference architecture** (experience → intelligence → integration → systems → data → knowledge plane, with identity/controls/FinOps cross-cutting); **ERP agnosticism as doctrine** (capability language, one system of record per data domain, the five-part core portability test, forbidden anti-patterns); **sourcing neutrality** (COTS vs built equivalence table — bought and built products carry identical lifecycle/security/control/exit obligations); the **AI-first doctrine** (design rules D1–D8, the hybrid workforce, the Tier-wired autonomy ladder with the canonical 1,384/3,269/758 splits, the four hard boundaries, interaction patterns P1–P5, headcount-as-model-output); the **agent factory** (paved-road components, the twelve-field Agent Charter, the six-stage lifecycle with owners and gates, the evaluation standard, cost-per-task value case, crawl-walk-run with workforce-transition rules); the **Enterprise Knowledge Base** (the ten-class canon table with owner + reference-instantiation columns, canonical/derived/consumption architecture, the citation-or-refusal read rule, the four-step write path, the ten-class consistency engine generalizing validate-repo.sh, agent memory rules incl. the write-back protocol, knowledge SLAs); the **no-confusion system** (the ownership rule across eight thing-classes, the decision-rights master table, the six-step Universal Decision Procedure with the defaults ladder, five escalation ladders with clocks, the twelve-row situation playbook index, the One-Page Card, and the closed-loop gap-admission flywheel); **governance cadence, layered KPIs, and the optimization loop**; and the **adoption path** (preconditions, seven phases with exit criteria, tailoring knobs incl. the 65–130 IT FTE band, the fifteen-point conformance test with item 9 non-waivable, the nine-entry anti-pattern catalogue).

**Content integration:** root-README intro sentence + folder-tree row added (Check 63(d) completeness); 07-methodology/README contents-table row added. No register, workflow, requirement, control, team, sizing, or phase changes — the guide cites the canonical figures (5,388 workflows / 5,411 register rows / Tiers 1,384+3,269+758 / 808 controls / 728 requirements / 69 validator checks / 17 teams / 122 FTE / TO 362→511 HQ and 6,762→6,911 total) and the live workflow-level owners (W5535 intake front door, W5515–W5517 sourcing & paved-road machinery, W5512–W5514 agent lifecycle) without altering any of them. `validate-repo.sh`: 0 errors / 0 warnings across all 69 checks.

## 2026-09-04 — Batch 15: capability demand-intake & backlog-triage gap fill — W5535 added as the workflow-level owner of the stakeholder capability-demand front door (the missing Stage 1–2 of the end-to-end new-capability cycle, upstream of the W5515 sourcing gate); canonical totals now 188 VS / 569 PA / 5,388 workflows / 5,411 register rows / 808 controls

**Gap filled.** The end-to-end cycle by which a stakeholder raises a new software-capability need — BPO/executive raises it with their paired IT PO/PM → the need is logged and triaged by EA against the 188-VS catalog (existing coverage vs. missing workflow-level owners vs. genuinely new value stream vs. sourcing candidate) → routed (team backlog, workflow-catalog gap-admission, or the W5515 SIB sourcing gate) → funded as capacity at the Product Council — had no dedicated workflow-level owner: every stage existed only as governance prose (OM §5.1/§7; sourcing model §3), with the downstream sourcing machinery itself owned since batch 7 by W5515–W5517. Keyword verification: 'capability demand intake'/'backlog triage' appear in zero dedicated `## W` headers — the adjacent VS-113 slices are each post-intake (W3577 portfolio rationalization, W3580 solution architecture for approved initiatives, W3588 investment-governance ROI, and the batch-7 machinery all start after intake).

**W5535 Capability Demand Intake & Backlog Triage** (PA-113.2, VS-113 — the application-portfolio front door it feeds): raise → log & classify (enhancement / gap-admission / sourcing candidate) → accepted/routed/declined-with-reason closed loop via the BPO → triage pack for sourcing candidates (BPO outcome statement & KPIs, IAP integration sketch per W3579, rough TCO per W4100, RA 10173/BIR compliance constraints, preliminary Tier proposal) → routing (team backlog / gap-analysis path / W5515 gate / PMO) → Product-Council funding (runs, not projects; >PHP 5M roadmap approval) → intake-register state feeding portfolio reporting (W3592) and the QBR. Ships **directly confirmed Tier 2** (the governance/lifecycle-operations class of its W5515/W5516 siblings; no statutory dimension — the front door routes and never executes statutory filings, matching the batch-7 precedent). Absorbed within the sized CIO Office (OM stays 122 FTE / 17 teams; TO stays HQ 511 / 6,911) — CIO Office workflow load 80 → 81; reconciliation now reads **171 + 17 = 188 / 4,878 + 510 = 5,388**. Register 5,410 → 5,411 rows (5,387 → 5,388 unique; T2 3,268 → 3,269; Tier 1/3 unchanged); `workflow-criticality-proposed.md` stays empty.

**Content integration:** sourcing-model §3 intro now names the front door explicitly (v1.9); OM gains the CIO-Office load true-up (v2.9); classification v7.50 note + Tier-2 Additions subsection + Summary batch-15 note; workflow-gap-analysis.md batch-15 note; dependency-map v4.21 (§8 intro block 2,755 → 2,756, VS-113 §8.3 row extended with the front-door wiring, §8.1 anchor table unchanged — the new workflow cites no top-10 anchors); touchpoint-map v90.0; semantic-audit-coverage registry gains the W5535 transition-path-pending note (admitted set unchanged at W1–W5517 = 5,370; pending now W5518–W5535 = 18; Check 69 closure live = admitted + pending holds). ## 2026-09-04 — Independent consistency review (fourth wave): the semantic-audit registry's batch-9 pending-note contradiction repaired (the detector audited-set silently read 5,374 — four never-audited workflows treated as swept), the two methodology tree rows stranded at the 5,367/5,370 era extended to the registry's true state, one stale Document-Relationships diagram figure trued, and the class guarded permanently by validator Check 69; canonical totals unchanged (188 VS / 569 PA / 5,387 workflows / 808 controls)

**Full independent re-derivation all exact (everything the 68 prior checks guard re-verified from raw files):** 188 VS dirs / 569 PA files / 5,387 unique `## W` headers; criticality register 5,410 distinct rows = 5,387 live + 23 letter-suffixed `###` sub-workflow rows, 0 ghosts, 0 missing (live ⊆ register bijection); tiers 1,384 + 3,268 + 758 = 5,410; workflows/README family subtotals re-derived per-VS from disk — 461 + 499 + 1,535 + 778 + 441 + 321 + 965 + 387 = 5,387, all 8 exact; requirements 728 rows = 724 plain + 4 letter-suffixed (POS-014a, NFR-022a, PUR-025a/b) IDs, all distinct, priority split 429 Must / 293 Should / 6 Nice, requirement-workflow-matrix bijection 728/728 exact; CTL register 808 distinct (cell-level 88 Preventive / 720 Detective); all 43 Python tools compile; W-id tokens in all 12 support/index/matrix docs resolve to live headers or register rows (0 ghosts); the two Check-51 informational hits adjudicated false positives of the non-guard elementwise parser (W537's sum chain 3×200 + 1×200 = 800; W1399's 200 × 13 = 2,600 store-department units with the ~40-category parenthetical an assortment-width gloss whose own 200 × 40 = 8,000 category-grain figure coheres).

**Defect 1 — semantic-audit registry self-contradiction (tool integrity).** The batch-9 pass (commit c56dc3c) appended its four workflow ids **W5525–W5528 as bare registry lines immediately above its own "NOT yet in the audited registry" pending note** — a contradiction no check read: `final-semantic-coverage.py` derives its audited set from those bare lines, so the detector pass silently treated four never-audited workflows as swept and reported **audited-set: 5,374** — matching neither the closed-loop 5,370 nor the live 5,387. Repaired by deleting the four stray lines; the file's admitted set is exactly W1–W5517 (5,370) and the tool now reports audited-set 5,370 with 0 flags. The second pending note's header range — which had also under-described its own body by remaining "W5525–W5528" after batches 10–11 extended that block's coverage — widened to **W5525–W5534** (people batch 9 / finance batch 10 / operations batch 11).

**Defect 2 — methodology tree rows stranded at the pre-batch-8 era (description-trueness).** The root-README tree's `semantic-audit-coverage.txt` row stopped at the W5512–W5514 admission ("→ 5,367 of 5,367") — omitting both the W5515–W5517 admission (→ 5,370 of 5,370, the file's own stated Total) and the 17 post-closure workflows; the `final-semantic-coverage.py` row stopped at the sourcing-model transition. Both rows extended to the registry's true current state: 5,370/5,370 admitted, with W5518–W5534 (the IT/people/finance/operations gap-fill passes, batches 8–11) carrying the registry's transition-path-pending notes — NOT yet admitted; admission follows the documented W5511 path (full read + D1–D4 detector sweep).

**Defect 3 — stale Document-Relationships figure.** The root-README ASCII cross-reference diagram still read `workflows/ (5,370 WF)` — last trued at the batch-7 sourcing-model pass and missed by every batch-8–14 re-point cascade (the diagram box is a surface no check reads; Check 44 guards the folder tree, not the relationship diagram). Trued to **5,387 WF**.

**Guard — validator Check 69 (semantic-audit registry integrity), check count 68 → 69.** Four invariants enforce the registry's line-list discipline (which IS the detector pass's coverage claim): (a) every bare W-id line distinct; (b) every bare W-id resolves to a live `## W` workflow header; (c) no W-id may appear both as a bare line and inside a `(transition-path pending)` note's declared en-dash range (the contradiction class itself); (d) the sets close — live workflows = admitted bare ids + pending-range ids, so an admission that forgets to retire its note or a note that forgets its ids both error (standalone prose mentions inside notes — e.g. "the documented W5511 transition path", which references the admitted exemplar — are not set membership). Teeth verified by three synthetic injections, each caught with correct diagnosis and the fixture restored byte-identical (sha256-verified): (i) the original defect re-injected (stray `W5525` bare line) → both the contradiction and the closure breach flagged; (ii) ghost bare id `W99999` → live-header resolution failure flagged; (iii) range drift `W5525–W5534` → `W5525–W5533` → closure error naming exactly `W5534`. Check-count self-descriptions re-pointed 68 → 69 (root-README tree line; 07-methodology/README validate-repo row extended with the new rule description). Canonical totals unchanged: **188 value streams / 569 process areas / 5,387 workflows / 728 requirements / 808 controls / 6,762 HC current — HQ 511 / 6,911 target; validator green 0 errors / 0 warnings across all 69 checks**

**Gap finding (workflow-granularity re-run of the §2 methodology against the rest of the 2026-09-03
hybrid capability-sourcing extension — sourcing model §3–§9, the surface batch 6's §12 scope
deliberately left unexamined):** the sourcing decision gate + Capability Sourcing Register, the
best-of-breed product lifecycle, and the SEP paved road/engineering standard were each defined as
a program (with named owners — SIB, Vendor Product Managers, SEP — in the model and the OM) but
owned by no workflow. The defining terms appeared in zero dedicated `## W` headers; 'exit
reserve'/'upgrade currency'/'golden path'/'ring deployment'/'DORA' in zero PA files at all; the
literal 'golden path'/'paved road'/'readiness review'/'DORA' hits that do exist are coincidental
('unpaved roads' in PA-09.1, 'staging-to-production readiness' in PA-10.1, a person's name in
PA-167.3).

**Three workflows created (one per VS-113 PA — hosted where the theme lives):**

- **W5515 Sourcing Decision Gate Operation & Capability Sourcing Register** (PA-113.3) — the §3
  gate and §4 Register: the scored configure → buy → build assessment (differentiation, ERP
  fit-to-standard gap, de-unification integration cost, data gravity, PH regulatory fit, 3-year
  TCO, talent/key-person, exit strategy; default order configure → buy → build); the five
  mandatory §3.3 appendices (IAP integration estimate, 808-control mapping appendix, FinOps-
  verified TCO sheet, exit/run-cost plan, re-evaluation trigger); ARB architecture opinion + SEC
  TPRM pre-read; the monthly SIB decision recorded in the Register by the vendor-portfolio
  analyst; decision-rights routing (3-year TCO > PHP 25M → Product Council ratification; Core-tier
  removal → CEO-noted waiver); annual QBR reaffirmation and re-evaluation-trigger monitoring.
- **W5516 Best-of-Breed Product Lifecycle Management, Vendor Release Intake & Exit Reserves**
  (PA-113.2) — the §8 lifecycle: vendor releases land in the owning team's staging ring and pass
  the intake checklist plus the domain regression pack (Tier-1 coverage mandatory) before
  production; defer one cycle, never two — the upgrade-currency KPI (at most one major version
  behind vendor current) with Tier & Control Board escalation at two behind; RA 10173 data-
  residency, statutory-readiness-warranty, exit/transition-assistance, open-format-export, and
  price-escalation-cap clauses verified at signature and each renewal; tier-1 annual TPRM
  reassessment (VS-161/W4729/W4734); QBR exit-reserve accrual into the CIO Office central bucket
  with QBR FinOps disclosure; vendor health/EOL and Register re-evaluation-trigger monitoring
  (W5515), retirement via W3578.
- **W5517 SEP Paved Road & Engineering Standard Governance for Built Products** (PA-113.1) — the
  §6/§7 standard enforced by the paved road rather than by memo: golden-path service templates
  (repo, CI/CD, observability, IaC) with ARB-recorded exceptions as the only deviation path;
  trunk-based development + feature flags; contract-first IAP integration with consumer-driven
  contract tests and DP data contracts; ring deployment (internal → canary stores/DCs → fleet)
  with automated SLO-burn rollback gates independent of the ERP monthly train; the security gates
  (SAST + dependency scan per merge, DAST before ring expansion, SBOM per release, secrets never
  in code) with the AppSec block right; the SEP + INFRA production readiness review before first
  fleet ring (typhoon-season resilience as a launch criterion); DORA-at-QBR reporting (deploy
  frequency ≥ weekly, lead time < 1 week, MTTR < 4 h, change-failure rate < 15%) plus Tier &
  Control Board sign-off for Tier-1-touching changes.

All three ship **directly confirmed Tier 2** — the governance/lifecycle-operations class of
their VS-113 siblings (W3588/W3589), with the contract-clause dimension carried inside W5516's
Step-3 verification gate (not a standalone statutory register, matching the W5513/W5511
precedent) — and are absorbed within the sized CIO Office, the four Vendor PM seats, and the SEP
platform team (OM stays 122 FTE / 17 teams; TO stays HQ 511 / 6,911).

Now Phase 1 **+18 → +11** (Legal +6, HSE +3, IA +2), Phase 2 **+26 → +20** (Finance +16, CS +4), Phase 3 **+105 → +118** with the ESG/Strategy/Trade top-ups made explicit (3→4, 3→4, 5→7 = +4 on top of the +114 named pairs); 11 + 20 + 118 = 149 = 511 − 362. New structural rules, teeth verified by synthetic injection (stale tier trio, inflated phase delta, and broken total all caught, then restored clean): **sourcing_tier_hits** re-derives the §12.1 ladder counts from the criticality register's Summary table on every run; **to_phase_hits** re-derives every §11 phase delta from its own named from→to moves and checks the three deltas against the stated total and the target-minus-current HQ range. ## 2026-09-03 — Agentic-AI extension: AI & Agent Platform (AAP) added as platform team #17 — governed agent automation of manual tasks on the VS-128 discipline — OM v2.1 (17 teams, 122 FTE), sourcing model v1.1 §12 (autonomy ladder, agent lifecycle), technical-guidelines v3.1 §6, TO v1.4 (HQ 511 / 6,911, band ~440–515), profile v2.27 — validator Check 59 anchors re-based to 66+56=122 / 511-6,911

The model company maximizes **agentic AI**: AI agents that execute manual tasks end-to-end inside guardrails, delivered by the owning product teams on a new platform team. The program rides structures that already exist — the 5,364-workflow Automation Opportunity inventory and Time Estimate data (the candidate backlog and its computable ROI), the IAP contract layer (agents act only through published contracts), the workflow Tier register (the autonomy ladder), VS-128's existing governance discipline (model registry, risk tiering, ethics review, kill-switch, AI incident management, RA 10173 automated-decision obligations), and the 17-team gap-free ownership invariant (every agent has exactly one accountable product team).

- **`it-product-operating-model.md` v2.1**: **AI & Agent Platform (AAP)** joins as platform team 15/17 (lead; 3 agent engineers; evaluation/AI-QA engineer; agent-ops SRE; AI-governance liaison dotted to VS-128/SEC — tool registry of IAP contracts only, guardrails + human-in-the-loop gates, offline→shadow→canary evaluation harness, non-human identity, kill-switch, cost telemetry); Design principle 8 ("agents are products"); SIB gains the AAP seat and agent-autonomy ratification; AAP KPI row (hours automated, task success, takeover rate, agent MTTR, cost-per-task); sizing re-based **115 → 122 FTE (66 + 56 = 122)**; phased build-up 50 → 122 (AAP stands up in Phase 2 with the crawl-phase read-only agents); agent anti-patterns added to §10.
- **`capability-sourcing-and-engineering-model.md` v1.1**: new **§12 Agentic Automation Program** (related docs renumbered to §13) — the **autonomy ladder wired to the workflow Tier register** (T1 human-approval-gated / T2 bounded autonomy / T3 autonomous-in-bounds) with hard boundaries (no agent owns a statutory filing; POS/OT exclusion; no SoD-conflicting agent roles); the agent lifecycle (candidate intake from the Automation Opportunity inventory + VS-133 process mining → SIB routing → VS-128 registration → shadow/canary evaluation → operation → QBR sunset); crawl-walk-run posture; workforce rules (VS-134 augmentation-first, VS-84 CBA sensitivity). Two register rows added: agentic runtime = **build** (AAP on bought foundation-model APIs); foundation-model access = **buy** under tier-1 TPRM (VS-161).
- **`technical-guidelines.md` v3.1**: new **§6 Agentic Runtime Reference** — tools-are-contracts, non-human identity & SOD, Tier-based autonomy ladder, statutory-boundary extension (human sign-off terminal on BIR/SSS/PhilHealth/Pag-IBIG filings), OT/POS exclusion, VS-128 registration & kill-switch, evaluation gates, audit-as-control-evidence (808-register mapping), privacy/DPIA rules, foundation-model vendors as tier-1 TPRM, per-agent cost telemetry with runaway-cost kill thresholds.
- **`optimal-table-of-organization.md` v1.4**: IT 115 → 122 (17 product teams); target HQ **504 → 511**, company total **6,904 → 6,911**; HQ band extended **~440–510 → ~440–515** (IT band 65–130 unchanged — 122 stays mid-band); §11 Phase 3 +98 → +105, total +142 → +149; §12 re-based (IT 122 → ~135 at 300 stores; HQ ~575); §8 SIB gains agent autonomy ratifications; revenue/employee ≈ PHP 9.01M.
- **`model-company-profile.md` v2.27**: §14.1 adds the AI & Agent Platform row; glossary adds "Agentic AI / AI Agent"; target-state notes re-based to HQ 511 / 6,911 (IT = 122).
- **`headcount-reality-check.md`**: 2026-09-03 update extended — HQ band tops at ~440–515, target HQ 511 / 6,911 with IT = 122; §3.2 agentic addendum (66 + 56, AAP +7, still mid-band).
- **`audit-model-docs.py` (Check 59)**: anchors re-based — OM sizing anchor **66+56=122**; TO **511/6,911**, ~440–515 band row, 504+7=511; the 115-era literals retired doc-scoped ("Steady-state design (115 IT FTE)", "16 product teams per", "IT 50→115", etc.). Executive-summary platform table and README trees updated.

Canonical totals unchanged: **188 VS / 569 PA / 5,364 WF / 728 Req / 808 CTL / 6,762 HC (current) · HQ 504 → 511 / 6,904 → 6,911 (target)** — the +7 target delta is the AAP platform team; all current-state figures and all non-IT department bands unchanged.

## 2026-09-03 — Hybrid capability-sourcing model adopted: best-of-breed edges + in-house builds around the unified ERP core — OM v2.0 (16 product teams, 115 FTE), new `capability-sourcing-and-engineering-model.md`, TO v1.3 (HQ 504 / 6,904), profile v2.26, technical-guidelines v3.0, reality-check band amendment — validator Check 59 anchors re-based

The single-vendor unified-ERP assumption underlying the IT operating model was replaced by a **hybrid capability-sourcing strategy**: the unified cloud ERP remains the **core** (financials, P2P, inventory ledger, HR/payroll, offline-capable POS, approvals — protecting the 5-day close, BIR/SSS statutory paths, the 808-control surface and the ≥ 8h offline POS architecture); four **best-of-breed** edges (WMS execution, TMS, store workforce management, field service management); two **in-house builds** for genuinely differentiating capabilities (OMO — omnichannel order orchestration, VS-60; TPS — trade & project services, VS-74/VS-77/VS-143).

- **New companion doc `07-methodology/capability-sourcing-and-engineering-model.md`** (v1.0): the Configure/Buy/Build decision gate (criteria scorecard; Sourcing & Investment Board — chaired by CIO, Head of EA runs assessment, >PHP 25M TCO to Product Council, core-tier waivers CEO-noted), the **Capability Sourcing Register** (initial issue: all seven decisions with re-evaluation triggers), the build-squad shape (PM, Tech Lead, 3–4 engineers, QA automation, shared UX), the **Software Engineering Platform (SEP)** paved-road definition (golden path, CI/CD, feature flags, ring deployment, SAST/DAST/SBOM gates, DORA targets), the BoB lifecycle (vendor PM, PH-localization & exit clauses, release-intake ring, upgrade-currency KPI, TPRM tiering, funded exit reserves) and funding/TCO rules (PFRS/IAS 38 capitalization with FIN).
- **`it-product-operating-model.md` v2.0**: portfolio 12 → **16 teams** — new products OMO (1 VS / 24 WF, from CCP) and TPS (3 VS / 72 WF, from WLI ×2 + CCP ×1) and platform team SEP; VS→team mapping updated (CCP 27 → 25 VS / 786 → 738 WF; WLI 22 → 20 VS / 571 → 523 WF; §4.9 reconciliation unchanged at **171 + 17 = 188 VS / 4,865 + 499 = 5,364 WF**); domain-team archetypes (configure / buy-and-integrate / build) with the Vendor Product Manager and build-squad roles (§5); sourcing + build-delivery RACI rows (§6.1, §6.2, new §6.4); **Sourcing & Investment Board** added to governance and ARB re-scoped from single-vendor waivers to core-tier waivers + sourcing opinions (§7); per-archetype release cadences replace the single monthly train — configure keeps the monthly train, BoB gets vendor-release intake rings, builds ship continuously behind feature flags (§8.2); OMO/TPS/SEP KPI rows incl. DORA (§8.3); sourcing/exit reserves + capitalization rules (§8.4); sizing re-based **80 → 115 FTE** (**66 + 49 = 115**, §9.1) against the hybrid need band, phased 50 → 115 (§9.3). IAP 5 → 9 (contract catalog + consumer-driven contract testing), SEC 5 → 7 (SDLC security + TPRM analyst), DP 5 → 7 (data-contract steward + dual-record MDM), CIO Office 3 → 4 (vendor-portfolio analyst).
- **`optimal-table-of-organization.md` v1.3**: IT target 80 → 115 (16 product teams); target HQ **469 → 504** and company total **6,869 → 6,904** (§1, §5.1: 497 + 7 = 504; revenue/employee ≈ PHP 9.02M); reality-check bands annotated (IT 65–80 → **65–130**; HQ ~440–470 → **~440–510**); §11 Phase 3 +63 → +98, total +107 → +142; §12 scaling re-based (IT 115 → ~130 at 300 stores; HQ ~565; company ~9,900); §8 adds the Sourcing & Investment Board; org-chart/§4.1/§5.2 IT blocks updated (9 domain + 6 platform teams).
- **`model-company-profile.md` v2.26**: §14.1 re-framed as the hybrid landscape (core rows + 4 BoB rows + 2 in-house rows); glossary "ERP Platform" scoped to the core + new "Best-of-Breed" entry; target-state notes re-based to HQ 504 / 6,904 (IT = 115). Current-state canon (§3.3/§4/§11.1: HQ 362, total 6,762, IT 50) unchanged.
- **`headcount-reality-check.md`**: 2026-09-03 amendment (STATUS banner + §3.2) — IT band extended 65–80 → 65–130 and HQ band ~440–470 → ~440–510 by the hybrid decision; the historical 65–80 gap record retained with the 14-role-family note (dev/DevOps now have permanent structural homes).
- **`technical-guidelines.md` v3.0**: new §5 multi-vendor sourcing-architecture reference — single integration path (IAP-only, no cross-product DB access), canonical contracts with consumer-driven tests, ERP as inventory ledger of record vs BoB WMS execution state, POS-offline preservation, SEP paved road for builds, vendor release-intake rings, statutory boundary (BIR/SSS filings stay on the core), DR posture for bought and built products.

README trees updated (new doc listed; OM row re-described).

Canonical totals unchanged: **188 VS / 569 PA / 5,364 WF / 728 Req / 808 CTL / 6,762 HC (current) · HQ 469 → 504 / 6,869 → 6,904 (target)** — the +35 target delta is entirely the IT hybrid revision; all current-state figures and all non-IT department bands unchanged.

## 2026-09-03 — Event-custody overlap pass: cross-cutting event routing register + typhoon triple-collision resolved + POS-returns routing closed + dependency-map self-loops repaired + gift-card escheat gap filled (W5511) — validator green 0/0 across 64 checks

A semantic-overlap review found the blind spot between the gap methodology (hunts capabilities in **zero** PA files) and validator Check 35 (verifies cited VS-numbers **resolve**): nothing detected the same capability governed by **multiple** value streams with conflicting authority. Worst case — the typhoon triple-collision: VS-26 (W848/W850/W1213/W1221/W1552), VS-24 (W695/W1223/W1386/W1387), and VS-69 (W2502–W2525) independently governed the same storm events with four different closure triggers (Signal 1 / Signal 2 / Signal 2+intensification / Signal 3+), four different commanders (Store Manager / Regional Manager / COO–Regional Ops Director / GSOC), two irreconciled frequency canons (5–10 vs 2–5 closures/store/yr), and **zero cross-references between VS-26 and VS-69**. Same class: POS returns were decided in VS-32 (W1622–W1630) while VS-08 (the POS catalog) never referenced VS-32, and VS-07 W12 carried a parallel cross-store threshold.

- **New support doc — [`event-custody-and-precedence-register.md`](01-model-company/workflows/event-custody-and-precedence-register.md):** 11-event custody table (accountable VS, canon workflow, supporting workflows, boundary note), the single PAGASA signal ladder (Signal 1 readiness → Signal 2 heightened prep, no closure → Signal 3+/LGU order/facility failure closure → all-clear assessment/reopening), the single incident-command ladder (Site IC = Store Manager → closure authority = Regional Manager per W2510, VP multi-store, COO chain-wide → corporate = CMT per W848 Step 5, GSOC hands off BCP-class command), frequency-canon reconciliation note (W850 all-cause 5–10/store/yr vs W2510 typhoon-signal 2–5/store/yr vs W848 5–8 chain activations/yr — all stand, scope-differentiated), and the declared-overlap-pair table (VS-24↔VS-26, VS-24↔VS-69, VS-26↔VS-69, VS-08↔VS-32, VS-07↔VS-32). COD cash custody (VS-142) and data-breach response (VS-91) documented as confirmed single-owner events.
- **Authority conflicts repaired in place:** W848 Step 3 now closes at Signal 3+/LGU order/facility safety failure *per the W2510 decision* (was Signal No. 2 — a direct trigger conflict with W2510); W850 Step 1's closure directive now issues from the Regional Manager per W2510 (VP multi-store, COO chain-wide; was "COO or Regional Manager" — ambiguous authority); VS-07 PA-07.2's "primary incident commander" staffing line now defers to the register's command ladder; VS-159 GSOC's "maintain incident command until handoff" names the W848 CMT as the BCP-class handoff target.

## 2026-09-01 — IT Product Operating Model issued (`07-methodology/it-product-operating-model.md`) — 12 product teams covering all 188 value streams; membership, roles, RACI, governance, sizing 50 → 80 FTE

First organizational artifact of the `07-methodology` folder: the target IT operating model for the post-go-live unified-ERP steady state.

- **Product taxonomy**: 7 stream-aligned domain teams (MSC Merchandising & Supply Chain Systems; WLI Warehouse, Logistics & Inventory Systems; SSP Store Systems & POS; CCP Commerce & Customer Platforms; FIN Finance & Treasury Systems; CORP Corporate, Governance & Asset Systems; PEO People Systems) + 5 platform teams (IAP Integration & API Platform; INFRA Cloud Infrastructure & SRE; SEC Cybersecurity, Privacy & OT Security; DP Data Platform & MDM; FS Field & End-User Services) + CIO Office (EA, portfolio governance, FinOps).
- **Full 188-VS assignment**: §4 maps every value stream to exactly one primary product team with its business process owner — the Governance & Assurance family (37 VS) gets no dedicated team (controls embedded in the owning products, coordinated by the SEC GRC cell with Internal Audit/Legal & Compliance as demand-side owners); the Technology & Data family (13 VS) is owned directly by the platform teams/CIO Office. §4.9 reconciliation sums exactly: 171 domain + 17 platform = 188 VS; 4,864 + 499 = 5,363 workflows (matches the value-stream-index grand totals).
- **Team membership & roles** (§5): standard 6-role domain-team core (IT Product Owner; Product/Process Architect; ERP Functional Analysts; CCP-only Commerce/App Engineer; Data & Reporting Analyst; QA & Release Analyst) with business-side membership (Business Product Owner + SMEs + shared Change & Training pool) explicitly excluded from IT headcount; platform-team rosters defined.
- **RACI** (§6): delivery, run-and-operate, and shared-object/control change matrices; single-A discipline throughout (house RACI key). **Governance** (§7): Product Council (monthly, CIO), Architecture Review Board (bi-weekly, Head of EA — custodian of the VS-113 unified-ERP principle), weekly Product Sync, monthly Tier & Control Board (808-control register, Internal Audit independence preserved per §11.1 note 1), quarterly QBR.
- **Sizing** (§9): steady state **80 FTE** (47 domain + 33 platform/CIO) vs the §3.3 current 50, the `headcount-reality-check.md` §3.2 need band 65–80, and the 100–168 industry benchmark — phase plan 50 → ~53 → ~70 → 80 prioritizing the gap-record's flagged archipelago field-support and integration-backbone shortfalls first.
- Root README tree + `07-methodology/README.md` contents updated. Canonical totals unchanged: **188 VS / 569 PA / 5,363 WF / 728 Req / 808 CTL / 6,762 HC**. `validate-repo.sh` green **0 errors / 0 warnings** across all **62 checks**.

## 2026-08-29 — Consistency review #59: semantic batch 15 (104 workflows, 1,513 → 1,617 of 5,363 read; 30.2% coverage) — 23 defect spots repaired across 13 classes: the "Amillaramento" misspelling (6-spot title cascade), phantom "PMBTI/NMIC" metrology acronyms, the DTI 24h-vs-48h recall-window conflict, and 9 arithmetic/canon classes

**Batch 15** (104 workflows, seed 5959, 13-per-family expansion-weighted, excluding all 1,513 prior W-ids; 78 distinct VS, 58 VS-53+, 98 PA files), pre-screened (zero flags) then read in full.

- **"Amillaramento" → "Amillaramiento" (6 spots, title cascade):** the Spanish-derived RPT term (origin of the Filipino "amilyar") was misspelled in the W119 and W430 titles — while the corpus's own requirements docs (GOV-003 in erp-requirements.md and the requirement matrix) already used the correct form. Web-verified against PH etymology sources; repaired through headers, TOC anchors, and both criticality-register rows; `fix-toc-anchors.py` rerun clean.
- **Phantom metrology acronyms removed (W3618 ×2):** the measurement-traceability chain cited "PMBTI for weights/measures" and "National Metrology Institute (PMBTI/NMIC)" — web-verified that no PH body named PMBTI exists (and NMIC is the meat-inspection commission); the PH national metrology institute is the **National Metrology Laboratory (NML) under DOST-ITDI** (ISO/IEC 17025-accredited via DAkkS; RA 9236 governs metrological controls). Both spots repaired.
- **DTI recall-window conflict resolved (W876 ×2):** W876 said "DTI: within 24 hours of recall decision" while W1618 (verified in batch 13) uses 48 hours three times — and web verification found RA 7394 prescribes **no hourly DTI deadline at all** (the 24/48/72-hour windows are FDA recall classes). Standardized on the W1618 48-hour corpus convention with the statutory basis noted.
- **Canon alignments (9 spots):** W42's count staffing "(30/store)" vs the 29-store-HC register and its own feasibility note ("~29 store staff"); W1399's "200 stores × 35 departments = ~7,000 forecasting units" vs the 13-department/~40-category register (×2 spots) → ~2,600 store-department units (up to ~8,000 at category grain); W1495's "~50–80 active VMI vendor partners" and "~200–350 VMI SKUs" vs the profile §6.4 / W1944 / W5495 canon of ~12 key vendors / ~300 SKUs; W1384's 13th-month population "~5,800 employees" vs the 6,762 total (×3 spots incl. the "PHP 5,800 employees" typo and the PHP 145M liability aligned to W1416's 150–200M); W1527's "~6,000 employees" background echo vs its own 6,762 register.
- **Aggregate/arithmetic (6 spots):** W868's three-way magnitude conflict (Frequency 200–300/month vs Volume 5,000–10,000/year vs 60 h/month staffing) trued to the Frequency basis (2,500–3,500/year; ~1,900–2,600 h/year; MDM ~100–150 h/month); W1105's "~8,500–9,500 invoices/month" vs its own 1,200–1,600/week and W1504's 5,000–8,000/month canon; W1182's "~1,000–1,200 hours/year" vs its itemized ~36-40 days → ~450–600; W1527's "PHP 8–12M" monthly statutory contributions re-derived at the actual rate stack (SSS 14% of MSC + PhilHealth 5% + Pag-IBIG ~3–4% on ~6,762 employees) → ~PHP 25–30M; W2180's "~1,400 hours/month" audit total vs its own 45-min×200×4 + 50-min×200 derivation → ~750–800; W270's "PHP 150,000–300,000/month" pallet-loss figure re-derived (75–150 losses × PHP 300–500) → ~PHP 25,000–75,000.

Teeth verified by synthetic injection (3 literals caught, restored clean) — **0 hits across all 569 PA files**.

## 2026-08-29 — Consistency review #57: dedicated placeholder-field pass — the review-#56 class discovery repaired to zero (173 missing-quantifier Volume/Frequency cells across 50 files), now guarded by a new structural Check 62 rule (teeth-verified)
